--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes E and N, we need to examine all paths between them.

1. **Node E Connections:**
   - E is connected to: A, B, S, F, B.

2. **Node N Connections:**
   - N is connected to: O, S.

3. **Neighboring Nodes of E:**
   - A: No connections to N.
   - B: No connections to N.
   - S: Connected to N.
   - F: No connections to N.

4. **Neighboring Nodes of S:**
   - E: Already considered.
   - N: Already considered.
   - O: No connections to E that are not through S.

5. **Paths between E and N:**
   - E → S → N
   - E → B → S → N
   - E → F → S → N
   - E → B → P → Q → I → J → R
   - E → F → Q → I → J → R
   - E → S → F → Q → I → J → R

To disconnect E and N, we need to remove the edges that form these paths. The minimal cut set can be identified by removing the following edges:

- (E, S)
- (E, F)
- (E, B)

However, removing (E, B) will also disconnect E → B → S → N. Therefore, we need to ensure that we remove the minimum number of edges while still disconnecting all paths.

By removing (E, S) and (E, F), we cut off the direct connections and indirect connections through S and F. This dislikcer won't disconnect E from N through B in these paths since E is not directly connected to N through B.

Therefore, the minimum number of edges to remove is 2:

- Remove (E, S)
- Remove (E, F)

This removal ensures that there are no more paths between E and N.

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(E, S), (E, F)].
